But I don't forget it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a serious head injury, a seizure, or been diagnosed with any neurological condition like epilepsy or a brain tumor?\n\nPatient: No... I haven't had anything like that. No head injuries or... seizures. Nothing like that.\n\nDoctor: Have friends or family mentioned that your personality has changed, or that you have become more impulsive or socially inappropriate recently?\n\nPatient: No... nobody has said anything like that to me. I just... I'm the same as I've always been, I think. I mostly just keep to myself anyway.\n\nDoctor: Before you tap the scanner or freeze, do you feel any kind of physical sensation or 'itch' in your body that is relieved only once you perform the action?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. It's not like an itch or... anything physical. I don't feel anything in my body before it happens. I just... do it.\n\nDoctor: When you are stuck at the register, does it feel like you are watching yourself from the outside or that you are somehow disconnected from your own body?\n\nPatient: No... not like that. I'm... I'm still me. I don't feel disconnected or... like I'm watching myself. I'm just there.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself repeating words or phrases under your breath, or making any small, repetitive movements like blinking or shrugging that you might have just become so used to that you don't even notice them anymore?\n\nPatient: No... I don't think so. I haven't noticed anything like that. Just... regular movements, I guess. I mean, just the usual aches from standing at the register all day, but... nothing strange. I just... I do what I need to do around the house. It's just normal stuff. I don't get stuck doing the same thing over and over. Just... regular routines.\n\nDoctor: Are you able to stop these movements if you really try to, or do you feel like your body is going to perform them regardless of your effort to stay still?\n\nPatient: I... I can't always. I don't know. I haven't really tried to do that. I just...
